TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; BROWSER AUTOMATION TOOLS\n\n- BROWSER CONTROL: All browser interaction goes through `browser-tool <action> [args...]` via `execute-command`.\n  * The browser (Chromium) is already running. Each command connects, acts, observes, and disconnects.\n\n- WORKFLOW (observe-act loop):\n  1. Run an action (e.g. navigate, click, fill). The tool automatically observes the page after every action.\n  2. Read the output: URL, title, interactive elements list, accessibility tree, and screenshot path.\n  3. Reference elements by their `[index]` number from the elements list (e.g. `click "[3]"`).\n  4. Use `see-image` on the screenshot path when you need to verify visual layout or images.\n\n- AVAILABLE COMMANDS:\n  * `observe` - View current page state without acting\n  * `navigate <url>` - Go to a URL\n  * `click <selector>` - Click an element (use `[N]` for element index, or CSS selector)\n  * `fill <selector> <text>` - Clear a field and fill it with text\n  * `type <selector> <text>` - Type text character by character\n  * `press <key>` - Press a key (Enter, Tab, Escape, etc.)\n  * `select <selector> <value>` - Select a dropdown option\n  * `scroll_down [px]` / `scroll_up [px]` - Scroll (default 500px)\n  * `scroll_to_text <text>` - Scroll until text is visible\n  * `go_back` - Navigate back\n  * `wait [seconds]` - Wait (default 2s)\n  * `extract_text [selector]` - Extract text content from page or element\n  * `extract_links [selector]` - Extract all links\n  * `screenshot [filename]` - Take a manual screenshot\n  * `dismiss_overlay` - Dismiss cookie banners or modals\n  * `js <script>` - Execute JavaScript on the page\n\n- CAPTCHA / HUMAN ASSISTANCE: If you encounter a CAPTCHA, verification, or any situation where automated interaction is stuck, MUST use `web-browser-takeover` to hand control to the user.\n\n# 12. js/pages/recommend.js\\n * Recommendation page logic:\\n *   - multi-select colour chips for shirt / pants / hat\\n *   - loads shops from /data/shops.json\\n *   - ranks shops by how many of the user\'s selected (category, colour)\\n *     picks each shop actually stocks\\n *   - the top shop is highlighted with the .is-best class\\n *\\n * No AI — pure deterministic filtering & sorting.\\n */\\n\\n\'use strict\';\\n\\n(function () {\\n  const COLORS      = [\'red\', \'blue\', \'yellow\'];\\n  const CATEGORIES  = [\'shirt\', \'pants\', \'hat\'];\\n  const COLOR_LABEL = { red: \'Red\', blue: \'Blue\', yellow: \'Yellow\' };\\n  const COLOR_RGB   = {\\n    red:    \'#e63946\',\\n    blue:   \'#2a6df4\',\\n    yellow: \'#f4c20d\'\\n  };\\n\\n  // selections[category] = Set of colours\\n  const selections = { shirt: new Set(), pants: new Set(), hat: new Set() };\\n\\n  let shops = [];\\n\\n  document.addEventListener(\'DOMContentLoaded\', async () => {\\n    try {\\n      const data = await Api.getShops();\\n      shops = data.shops || [];\\n    } catch (_) {\\n      document.getElementById(\'results\').innerHTML =\\n        \'<p class=\\"text-muted\\">Could not load shops.</p>\';\\n      return;\\n    }\\n\\n    renderChips();\\n    wireControls();\\n    update(); // initial render (empty state)\\n  });\\n\\n  /* ---------- Chip rendering ---------- */\\n  function renderChips() {\\n    CATEGORIES.forEach((cat) => {\\n      const host = document.getElementById(\'chips-\' + cat);\\n      if (!host) return;\\n      host.innerHTML = COLORS.map((color) => chipHtml(cat, color)).join(\'\');\\n    });\\n\\n    // delegate clicks across the picker\\n    document.querySelector(\'.picker\').addEventListener(\'click\', (e) => {\\n      const chip = e.target.closest(\'.chip\');\\n      if (!chip) return;\\n      const cat   = chip.dataset.category;\\n      const color = chip.dataset.color;\\n      const set   = selections[cat];\\n      if (set.has(color)) set.delete(color);\\n      else set.add(color);\\n      chip.classList.toggle(\'is-selected\');\\n      update();\\n    });\\n  }\\n\\n  function chipHtml(cat, color) {\\n    return (\\n      \'<button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"chip\\" data-category=\\"\' + cat + \'\\" data-color=\\"\' + color + \'\\">\' +\\n        \'<span class=\\"chip__dot\\" style=\\"background:\' + COLOR_RGB[color] + \'\\"></span>\' +\\n        COLOR_LABEL[color] +\\n      \'</button>\'\\n    );\\n  }\\n\\n  /* ---------- Clear / Select all ---------- */\\n  function wireControls() {\\n    const clear = document.getElementById(\'clearBtn\');\\n    if (clear) clear.addEventListener(\'click\', () => {\\n      CATEGORIES.forEach((c) => selections[c].clear());\\n      document.querySelectorAll(\'.chip.is-selected\').forEach((el) => el.classList.remove(\'is-selected\'));\\n      update();\\n    });\\n\\n    const all = document.getElementById(\'selectAllBtn\');\\n    if (all) all.addEventListener(\'click\', () => {\\n      CATEGORIES.forEach((c) => {\\n        COLORS.forEach((color) => selections[c].add(color));\\n      });\\n      document.querySelectorAll(\'.chip\').forEach((el) => el.classList.add(\'is-selected\'));\\n      update();\\n    });\\n  }\\n\\n  /* ---------- Ranking ---------- */\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Count how many of the user\'s picks a shop satisfies.\\n   * A pick = { category, color }. A shop satisfies it if it has an\\n   * item with the same category AND color.\\n   */\\n  function scoreShop(shop) {\\n    let matched = 0;\\n    const matches = []; // list of { category, color, itemName }\\n\\n    CATEGORIES.forEach((cat) => {\\n      selections[cat].forEach((color) => {\\n        const item = shop.items.find((it) => it.category === cat && it.color === color);\\n        if (item) {\\n          matched++;\\n          matches.push({ category: cat, color, name: item.name, price: item.price });\\n        }\\n      });\\n    });\\n\\n    return { shop, matched, total: totalPicks(), matches };\\n  }\\n\\n  function totalPicks() {\\n    return CATEGORIES.reduce((sum, c) => sum + selections[c].size, 0);\\n  }\\n\\n  /* ---------- Render ---------- */\\n  function update() {\\n    // Summary line\\n    const total = totalPicks();\\n    const summary = document.getElementById(\'pickSummary\');\\n    if (summary) {\\n      summary.textContent = total === 0\\n        ? Built as a university thesis project and the foundation for a future startup. **This is not an e-commerce site** — it is an outfit design and recommendation platform.\\n\\n## Features\\n\\n- **User accounts** — register, log in, log out (bcrypt password hashing, express-session authentication, SQLite storage).\\n- **Dashboard** — personalised greeting and navigation cards to every tool.\\n- **Avatar Designer** — a layered 2D PNG avatar (head / body / legs / hat). Pick a colour (red, blue, yellow) for each garment layer and see the preview update instantly. No AI, no 3D — pure 2D image compositing.\\n- **Outfit Designer** — combine a shirt, pants and hat into a named outfit. Save it to your wardrobe (stored in SQLite with the outfit name, date and colour choices). Edit existing outfits too.\\n- **Wardrobe** — browse every saved outfit as a card with a live avatar preview, colour swatches, and Edit / Duplicate / Delete actions.\\n- **Shop** — three demo shops, each themed around a single colour (Shop A = red, Shop B = blue, Shop C = yellow). Browse shirts, pants and hats. No checkout — this is a browsing experience.\\n- **Recommendations** — multi-select the colours you want for shirt, pants and hat. The app ranks every shop by how many of your picks it stocks and highlights the best match. No AI — a simple, transparent colour/category matching filter.\\n- **Profile** — view your username, email, avatar, member-since date and body measurements (placeholder). Edit your profile through a modal.\\n\\n## Tech Stack\\n\\n| Layer        | Technology                                      |\\n| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------- |\\n| Frontend     | HTML5, CSS3 (custom properties), Vanilla JS    |\\n| Backend      | Node.js, Express.js                             |\\n| Database     | SQLite (via the `sqlite3` package)              |\\n| Auth         | `bcrypt` (password hashing), `express-session`  |\\n| Avatar assets| 2D PNG layers generated from SVG (Python cairosvg script included) |\\n\\nNo frontend framework. All interactivity is progressive enhancement on top of working HTML forms.\\n\\n## Project Structure\\n\\n```\\ntryiton/\\n├── app.js                      # Express entry point (middleware, routes, server)\\n├── package.json                # Dependencies and npm scripts\\n├── config/\\n│   └── index.js                # Central config (port, db path, session, bcrypt rounds)\\n├── db/\\n│   ├── connection.js           # SQLite singleton (promisified run/get/all)\\n│   └── init.js                 # Schema creation (users, outfits, sessions)\\n│   └── tryiton.sqlite          # Generated database file (created on init)\\n├── middleware/\\n│   ├── auth.js                 # requireAuth, redirectIfAuth, attachUser\\n│   ├── validate.js             # Registration, login, outfit input validators\\n│   └── errorHandler.js         # 404 + 500 error handlers\\n├── models/\\n│   ├── user.js                 # create, findByEmail, findById, verifyPassword…\\n│   └── outfit.js               # create, findByUser, update, remove, findById\\n├── controllers/\\n│   ├── authController.js       # register, login, logout\\n│   ├── profileController.js    # show profile, get/update /api/me\\n│   └── outfitController.js     # list, create, update, remove, duplicate\\n├── routes/\\n│   ├── index.js                # GET / → landing page\\n│   ├── auth.js                 # /auth/register, /auth/login, /auth/logout\\n│   ├── pages.js                # Protected page routes (dashboard, design, …)\\n│   └── api.js                  # JSON API (/api/me, /api/outfits…)\\n├── generate_avatar_assets.py   # Python script that renders SVG → PNG layers\\n├── public/                     # Static assets served by Express\\n│   ├── index.html              # Landing page\\n│   ├── register.html, login.html\\n│   ├── dashboard.html, profile.html\\n│   ├── avatar.html, design.html\\n│   ├── wardrobe.html, shop.html\\n│   ├── recommend.html\\n│   ├── 404.html, 500.html\\n│   ├── css/                    # variables, base, navbar, buttons, cards,\\n│   │                           #   forms, avatar, animations, utilities,\\n│   │                           #   landing, pages\\n│   ├── js/\\n│   │   ├── utils.js            # formatDate, escape, toast, sleep, param\\n│   │   ├── api.js              # fetch wrapper for the JSON API\\n│   │   ├── main.js             # Navbar init, scroll reveals, footer\\n│   │   ├── components/\\n│   │   │   ├── navbar.js       # Shared navbar (injected per page)\\n│   │   │   └── avatar.js       # Layered 2D avatar renderer\\n│   │   └── pages/              # One script per page\\n│   │       ├── landing.js, auth.js, dashboard.js, profile.js,\\n│   │       ├── avatar.js, design.js, wardrobe.js, shop.js, recommend.js\\n│   ├── images/avatar/          # PNG avatar layers + logo\\n│   └── data/shops.json         # Static demo shop data\\n└── README.md\\n```\\n\\n## Getting Started\\n\\n### Prerequisites\\n\\n- **Node.js** 18 or newer (tested on Node 20)\\n- **npm** (comes with Node)\\n- *(optional)* Python 3 with `cairosvg` — only needed if you want to regenerate the avatar PNGs. The pre-rendered PNGs are already included in `public/images/avatar/`.\\n\\n### Installation\\n\\n1. Install dependencies:\\n\\n   ```bash\\n   npm install\\n   ```\\n\\n2. Initialise the SQLite database (creates `db/tryiton.sqlite` with the `users`, `outfits` and `sessions` tables):\\n\\n   ```bash\\n   npm run init-db\\n   ```\\n\\n3. Start the server:\\n\\n   ```bash\\n   npm start\\n   ```\\n\\n4. Open the app in your browser:\\n\\n   ```\\n   http://localhost:3000\\n   ```\\n\\n### npm Scripts\\n\\n| Script         | Description                                   |\\n| -------------- | --------------------------------------------- |\\n| `npm start`    | Start the production server (`node app.js`)   |\\n| `npm run dev`  | Start the server (alias of `npm start`)       |\\n| `npm run init-db` | Create / reset the SQLite database schema  |\\n\\n## Database Schema\\n\\nThe database is created automatically by `npm run init-db`. Three tables:\\n\\n```sql\\n-- users\\nCREATE TABLE users (\\n  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,\\n  email      TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,\\n  username   TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,\\n  password   TEXT NOT NULL,        -- bcrypt hash\\n  avatar     TEXT,                  -- JSON of avatar colours\\n  measurements TEXT,               -- JSON of body measurements\\n  created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime(\'now\'))\\n);\\n\\n-- outfits\\nCREATE TABLE outfits (\\n  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,\\n  user_id    INTEGER NOT NULL,\\n  name       TEXT NOT NULL,\\n  shirt      TEXT NOT NULL,        -- \'red\' | \'blue\' | \'yellow\'\\n  pants      TEXT NOT NULL,\\n  hat        TEXT NOT NULL,\\n  created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime(\'now\')),\\n  FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id)\\n);\\n\\n-- sessions (express-session table-backed store is optional;\\n-- express-session uses the MemoryStore by default in this PoC)\\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (\\n  sid  TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\\n  sess TEXT NOT NULL,\\n  expired INTEGER\\n);\\n```\\n\\n## API Reference\\n\\nAll `/api/*` routes require an authenticated session (returns `401` JSON if not logged in).\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint                  | Description                          |\\n| ------ | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |\\n| GET    | `/api/me`                 | Current user\'s profile data          |\\n| POST   | `/api/me`                 | Update username and/or measurements  |\\n| GET    | `/api/outfits`            | List the user\'s saved absolutely with z-index ordering:\\n\\n```\\nz-index (bottom → top):  legs  →  body  →  head  →  hat\\n```\\n\\n- **head.png** — a single shared head (no colour variant).\\n- **body-{color}.png** — swaps the `src` of one `<img>` layer. A CSS `avatar-pop` keyframe re-triggers on every swap for a subtle animation. The PNGs were generated from SVG sources using `generate_avatar_assets.py` (Python + `cairosvg`).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧭 Page Guide\\n\\n| Page            | Route        | Auth | Purpose Rounded corners, soft shadows, glassmorphism, scroll-reveal animations, and a fully responsive grid (desktop-first, mobile-friendly).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧱 Tech stack\\n\\n- **Frontend:** HTML5, CSS3 (custom properties, no preprocessor), Vanilla JavaScript (ES6+, no framework)\\n- **Backend:** Node.js + Express.js\\n- **Database:** SQLite (`sqlite3`)\\n- **Auth:** `bcrypt` (password hashing) + `express-session` (session cookies)\\n- **Avatar:** 2D layered PNG compositing (head / body / legs / hat) — **no AI, no 3D**\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📁 Project structure\\n\\n```\\ntryiton/\\n├── app.js                      # Express entry point (middleware + routes + error handling)\\n├── package.json                # Scripts & dependencies\\n├── config/\\n│   └── index.js                # Central config (port, db path, session secret, bcrypt rounds)\\n├── db/\\n│   ├── connection.js           # /api/me, /api/outfits (CRUD + duplicate)\\n├── generate_avatar_assets.py   # Python script that renders the avatar PNGs from SVG (cairosvg)\\n└── public/                     # Everything served as static files\\n    ├── index.html, register.html, login.html, dashboard.html,\\n    │   profile.html, avatar.html, design.html, wardrobe.html,\\n    │   shop.html, recommend.html, 404.html, 500.html\\n    ├── css/\\n    │   ├── variables.css       # .btn variants (primary, ghost, lg, sm, block)\\n    │   ├── cards.css           # .card, .glass, .nav-card, .outfit-card, .swatch\\n    │   ├── forms.css           # .field, .field__input, validation states\\n    │   ├── avatar.css          # managed by connect-sqlite3-style usage)\\nCREATE TABLE sessions (\\n  sid  TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\\n  sess TEXT NOT NULL,\\n  expired INTEGER\\n);\\n```\\n\\n> Passwords are hashed with **bcrypt (10 rounds)** before storage. Session cookies are HTTP-only and expire after 24 hours.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🎨 The avatar system (no AI, no 3D)\\n\\nThe avatar is four transparent PNG layers stacked with `z-index`:\\n\\n```\\nlegs  →  body  →  head  →  hat   (bottom to top)\\n```\\n\\nEach colour-specific layer (`body-red.png`, `legs-blue.png`, etc.) is a 600×840 transparent PNG generated from SVG via `generate_avatar_assets.py`. Changing a colour is as simple as swapping one `<img>`\'s `src` — the change is instant and animated with a CSS `avatar-pop` keyframe.\\n\\nColours chosen in the **Avatar Designer** persist in `localStorage` (`tryiton.avatar`) so the **Outfit Designer** opens with the same palette.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧪 Smoke test checklist\\n\\nOnce the server is running, verify the full flow:\\n\\n1. **Register** a new user at `/auth/register`.\\n2. **Login** at `/auth/login`.\\n3. **Dashboard** greets you by username.\\n4. **Avatar Designer** — pick colours; the avatar updates live.\\n5. **Outfit Designer** — name and save an outfit.\\n6. **Wardrobe** — your saved outfit appears; try **Duplicate** and **Delete**.\\n7. **Wardrobe → Edit** — returns to the Outfit Designer in edit mode; save changes.\\n8. **Shop** — switch between the three colour-themed shops.\\n9. **Recommendations** — select colours per category; shops re-rank live; the best match is highlighted.\\n10. **Profile** — edit your username / measurements in the modal.\\n11. **Logout** — returns to the landing page.\\n12. Visit a bogus URL (e.g. `/nope`) — the custom **404** page appears.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📝 Notes for the thesis / startup\\n\\n- The codebase is intentionally **framework-free** on the front end to keep it readable and dependency-light — ideal for academic review.\\n- Backend follows a light **MVC** structure: `routes → controllers → models → db`, with reusable middleware for auth, validation, and errors.\\n- The Recommendation engine is deliberately transparent (a set-intersection score) rather than a black-box model, so the \\"why\\" behind every ranking is explainable — a useful talking point for the thesis.\\n- All avatar assets are procedurally generated (SVG → PNG), so new colours or shapes can be added by editing one Python script.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📄 License\\n\\nMIT — see `package.json`.\\n", "file_path": "README.md"}'}}]}

Browse only — no checkout.\\n- **Recommendations** — multi-select colours for each item category; the app ranks every shop by how many of your picks it stocks and highlights the best match. No AI — pure filtering.\\n- **Profile** — view your account details and edit your username and body measurements.\\n- **Custom error pages** — friendly 404 and 500 pages.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Tech Stack\\n\\n| Layer        | Technology                                      |\\n| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------- |\\n| Frontend     | HTML5, CSS3 (custom properties), Vanilla JS (ES6+) |\\n| Backend      | Node.js, Express.js                             |\\n| Database     | SQLite (via the `sqlite3` npm package)          |\\n| Auth         | `bcrypt` (password hashing), `express-session`  |\\n| Avatar art   | 2D PNG layers generated from SVG via Python `cairosvg` |\\n\\nNo front-end framework, no build step, no bundler. The app runs exactly as written.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Project Structure\\n\\n```\\ntryiton/\\n├── app.js                      # Express entry point — middleware, routes, server\\n├── package.json                # Dependencies + npm scripts\\n├── README.md                   # This file\\n├── generate_avatar_assets.py   # Python script that builds the avatar PNGs (SVG → PNG)\\n│\\n├── config/\\n│   └── index.js                # Central config: port, DB path, session, bcrypt rounds\\n│\\n├── db/\\n│   ├── connection.js           # SQLite singleton + promisified run/get/all helpers\\n│   ├── init.js                 # Schema creation + seed-safe startup\\n│   └── tryiton.sqlite          # The database file (auto-created on first run)\\n│\\n├── models/\\n│   ├── user.js                 # User CRUD + bcrypt hashing + profile updates\\n│   └── outfit.js               # Outfit CRUD (scoped to a user)\\n│\\n├── middleware/\\n│   ├── auth.js                 # requireAuth, redirectIfAuth, attachUser\\n│   ├── validate.js             # Registration / login / outfit input validators\\n│   └── errorHandler.js         # 404 + 500 handler (JSON for /api routes)\\n│\\n├── controllers/\\n│   ├── authController.js       # showRegister, register, showLogin, login, logout\\n│   ├── profileController.js    # show profile, GET/POST /api/me\\n│   └── outfitController.js     # list, create, update, remove, duplicate outfits\\n│\\n├── routes/\\n│   ├── index.js                # GET / → landing page\\n│   ├── auth.js                 # /auth/register, /auth/login, /auth/logout\\n│   ├── pages.js                # Protected pages: /dashboard, /profile, /avatar, ...\\n│   └── api.js                  # JSON API: /api/me, /api/outfits, /api/outfits/:id\\n│\\n└── public/                     # Everything served statically at the site root\\n    ├── index.html              # Landing page\\n    ├── register.html           # Register page\\n    ├── login.html              # Login page\\n    ├── dashboard.html          # Dashboard\\n    ├── profile.html            # Profile + edit modal\\n    ├── avatar.html             # Avatar Designer\\n    ├── design.html             # Outfit Designer (create + edit)\\n    ├── wardrobe.html           # Wardrobe (saved outfits)\\n    ├── shop.html               # Shop browser\\n    ├── recommend.html          # Recommendations\\n    ├── 404.html                # Not-found error page\\n    ├── 500.html                # Server-error page\\n    │\\n    ├── css/\\n    │   ├── variables.css       # Design tokens (colours, type scale, spacing, motion)\\n    │   ├── base.css            # Reset + base typography + layout helpers\\n    │   ├── navbar.css          # Sticky glassmorphism navbar\\n    │   ├── buttons.css         # Button variants\\n    │   ├── cards.css           # Card + outfit-card + swatch components\\n    │   ├── forms.css           # Form fields + validation states\\n    │   ├── avatar.css          # Layered avatar stage + pop animation\\n    │   ├── animations.css      # Keyframes + reveal-on-scroll\\n    │   ├── utilities.css       # Small utility classes (badge, text-muted, flex...)\\n    │   ├── landing.css         # Landing-page-specific styles\\n    │   └── pages.css           # Dashboard, designer, wardrobe, shop, recommend, modal\\n    │\\n    ├── js/\\n    │   ├── utils.js            # formatDate, param, escape (XSS), toast, sleep\\n    │   ├── api.js              # Fetch wrapper + all API call helpers\\n    │   ├── main.js             # Navbar init, page-enter, scroll reveals, footer\\n    │   ├── components/\\n    │   │   ├── navbar.js       # Shared navbar (injects into #navbar per page)\\n    │   │   └── avatar.js       # Avatar.render() — stacked 2D PNG compositing\\n    │   └── pages/\\n    │       ├── landing.js      # Hero avatar + interactive demo\\n    │       ├── auth.js         # Shared register/login logic\\n    │       ├── dashboard.js    # Greeting personalisation\\n    │       ├── profile.js      # Profile rendering + edit modal\\n    │       ├── avatar.js       # Avatar Designer logic\\n    │       ├── design.js       # Outfit Designer (create + edit modes)\\n    │       ├── wardrobe.js     # Wardrobe grid + actions\\n    │       ├── shop.js         # Shop tabs + item rendering\\n    │       └── recommend.js    # Multi-select chip picker + shop ranking\\n    │\\n    ├── data/\\n    │   └── shops.json          # Static demo shop data (3 shops, 9 items)\\n    │\\n    └── images/\\n        └── avatar/             # All 600×840 PNG layers + logo\\n            ├── head.png\\n            ├── body-red.png, body-blue.png, body-yellow.png\\n            ├── legs-red.png, legs-blue.png, legs-yellow.png\\n            ├── hat-red.png,  hat-blue.png,  hat-yellow.png\\n            └── logo.png\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Prerequisites\\n\\n- **Node.js** v18 or newer (developed on Node 20)\\n- **npm** (comes with Node)\\n- Python 3 is **only** needed if you want to regenerate the avatar PNGs (they are already included in `public/images/avatar/`). It requires the `cairosvg` package: `pip install cairosvg`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Getting Started\\n\\n### 1. Install dependencies\\n\\n```bash\\nnpm install\\n```\\n\\nThis installs Express, bcrypt, express-session, and sqlite3.\\n\\n### 2. Initialise the database\\n\\n```bash\\nnpm run init-db\\n```\\n\\nThis creates `db/tryiton.sqlite` with three tables:\\n\\n| Table     | Purpose                                                        |\\n| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |\\n| `users`   | id, email (unique), username (unique), password_hash, measurements, created_at |\\n| `outfits` | id, user_id (FK), name, shirt, pants, hat, created_at, updated_at |\\n| `sessions`| Reserved for future persistent session storage                |\\n\\n> The init script is safe to run multiple times — it uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`.\\n\\n### 3. Start the server\\n\\n```bash\\nnpm start\\n```\\n\\nYou should see:\\n\\n```\\n[TryItOn!] Server running → http://localhost:3000\\n```\\n\\n### 4. Open the app\\n\\nVisit **http://localhost:3000** in your browser.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Usage Walkthrough\\n\\n1. **Register** an account on the Register page (email, username, password, confirm password).\\n2. **Log in** with your new credentials. You\'ll be redirected to the Dashboard.\\n3. Open the **Avatar Designer** and pick colours for your shirt, pants, and hat. The avatar updates instantly. Your choices are saved to `localStorage` so they carry over to the Outfit Designer.\\n4. Go to the **Outfit Designer**, adjust colours if you like, name your outfit, and click **Save outfit**.\\n5. Visit the **Wardrobe** to see your saved outfit. From here you can Edit, Duplicate, or Delete it.\\n6. Browse the **Shop** to see three colour-themed demo shops.\\n7. Open **Recommendations**, multi-select colours per category, and watch the shops get ranked by how many of your picks they stock.\\n8. View and edit your **Profile** (username + body measurements) via the edit modal.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## API Reference\\n\\nAll `/api` routes require an authenticated session. Unauthenticated requests receive `401`.\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint                    | Description                          |\\n| ------ | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |\\n| GET    | `/api/me`                   | Current user\'s public profile        |\\n| POST   | `/api/me`                   | Update username / measurements       |\\n| GET    | `/api/outfits`              | List current user\'s outfits (newest first) |\\n| POST   | `/api/outfits`              | Create a new outfit                  |\\n| PUT    | `/api/outfits/:id`          | Update an outfit (name + colours)    |\\n| DELETE | `/api/outfits/:id`          | Delete an outfit                     |\\n| POST   | `/api/outfits/:id/duplicate`| Duplicate an outfit                  |\\n\\n### Auth routes (form-based, not JSON)\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint            | Description              |\\n| ------ | ------------------- | ------------------------ |\\n| GET    | `/auth/register`    | Render register page     |\\n| POST   | `/auth/register`    | Create account           |\\n| GET    | `/auth/login`       | Render login page        |\\n| POST   | `/auth/login`       | Log in (create session)  |\\n| POST   | `/auth/logout`      | Destroy session          |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## How the Avatar Works\\n\\nThe avatar is a purely 2D, layered PNG composite — **no AI, no 3D**. Four transparent PNGs, each 600×840 pixels, are stacked with absolute positioning and controlled by `z-index`:\\n\\n```\\nz-index order (bottom → top):  legs  →  body  →  head  →  hat\\n```\\n\\n- `legs-{color}.png` — the pants\\n- `body-{color}.png` — the shirt\\n- `head.png` — a single neutral head (shared across all outfits)\\n- `hat-{color}.png` — the hat\\n\\nChanging a colour is as simple as swapping one `<img>`\'s `src`. A CSS `avatar-pop` keyframe gives the changed layer a subtle scale bounce. Because the layers are pixel-aligned, the composite always looks seamless.\\n\\nTo regenerate the PNGs from scratch (optional):\\n\\n```bash\\npython generate_avatar_assets.py\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Design System\\n\\nThe visual language is defined entirely by CSS custom properties in `public/css/variables.css`:\\n\\n- **Colours:** white, black, light gray, accent blue, plus semantic red/blue/yellow for avatar items.\\n- **Typography:** a modular type scale using `clamp()` for fluid sizing.\\n- **Spacing:** a consistent spacing scale (`--space-1` … `--space-8`).\\n- **Radii & shadows:** rounded corners and soft shadows for a modern, tactile feel.\\n- **Motion:** duration and easing variables for consistent transitions.\\n\\nAdditional patterns: glassmorphism (`backdrop-filter: blur()`), scroll-reveal animations via `IntersectionObserver`, and a responsive grid that collapses gracefully on mobile.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Configuration\\n\\nAll tunable values live in `config/index.js` and can be overridden with environment variables:\\n\\n| Variable         | Default                  | Purpose                        |\\n| ---------------- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------ |\\n| `PORT`           | `3000`                   | HTTP port                      |\\n| `SESSION_SECRET` | `tryiton-dev-secret-...` | Session signing secret (**change in production**) |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Security Notes\\n\\n- Passwords are hashed with **bcrypt** (10 rounds).\\n- Sessions are managed with **express-session** using HTTP-only cookies.\\n- All user-supplied text rendered into the DOM is escaped via `Utils.escape()` to prevent XSS.\\n- Input validation middleware guards registration, login, and outfit creation.\\n- Outfit CRUD is strictly scoped to the authenticated user\'s ID — you can never read or modify another user\'s outfits.\\n\\n> **For production:** set a strong `SESSION_SECRET`, enable `secure: true` on the session cookie (behind HTTPS), and consider a persistent session store (e.g. `connect-sqlite3`).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Scripts\\n\\n| Command            | Description                          |\\n| ------------------ | ------------------------------------ |\\n| `npm start`        | Start the production server          |\\n| `npm run dev`      | Start the server (alias of start)    |\\n| `npm run init-db`  | Create the SQLite database & tables  |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## License\\n\\nMIT — free to use for educational and commercial purposes.\\n", "file_path": "README.md"}'}}]}
